## 1. The 12 Oct arrests are the **Rhône** — and Strasbourg says 13 too

The AFP-sourced chronology states that on 12 October, in the Rhône, the prefecture counted **27 establishments affected (19 lycées, 6 universities, 2 colleges) and 13 interpellations** [^1].

But independently, France 3 Grand Est reported the Bas-Rhin prefecture's figure for the same day: **"Selon la préfecture, elle a procédé à 13 interpellations dans la journée"**, including four people preparing incendiary devices, three for mortar fire and one for "dégradation d'un véhicule de police par jets de projectiles" [^2]. The article is timestamped 12/10/2026 12h09, updated 19h48 [^2], and describes blockades at Fustel de Coulanges, Marie-Curie, Kléber, Jean-Monnet and Les Pontonniers, with **about ten mobile-gendarmerie lorries** stationed along the schools [^2].

So the cell value 13 is right, but its provenance was wrong in both directions:

- The old `obsNotes` said **Paris** — wrong.
- My September pass said **Rhône** — right, but incomplete, because there are **two** prefectures reporting 13.

Two prefectures publishing the identical number on the same day is either coincidence or a transcription collision in the press. Either way, **13 is a floor for the day, not the day's regional total** — if both counts are independent, the true regional figure is 26.

**Recommendation:** keep the cell at 13 with flag `r`, and make the note say so explicitly. Do *not* raise it to a 13–26 band — that would invent an upper bound.

---

## 2. The €1.5M is **Normandy**, it was announced on **12 October**, and it is a **stock**

This is the biggest correction of the pass. Hervé Morin, president of the Normandy regional council, addressing the regional assembly at Rouen on **Monday 12 October**, gave the first regional tally: *"le coût des dégradations occasionnées en marge des manifestations ou des blocages atteint d'ores et déjà 30 millions d'euros, dont **1,5 million en Normandie**"* [^3].

Three consequences:

1. **The €1.5M is Normandy** — regional, published by the région, not a local estimate. The old note ("the Ardèche prefecture reported no damage, so it comes from elsewhere") is now superseded.
2. **The date is 12 October, not 13 October.** The cell must move.
3. **It is a cumulative stock** — "atteint d'ores et déjà" — so the flag must change from `e` (derived) to `c` (cumulative/subset), matching the Île-de-France precedent and the 18 Oct Paris subset.

### Four damage figures the dataset never had

| Date | Figure | Scope | Basis | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 Oct | **€30M** | national | CNEWS's own compilation; no ministry published a national tally [^4] | **New cell** |
| 10 Oct | €7M | Île-de-France | cited by CNEWS from Pécresse [^4] | Cross-check |
| 12 Oct | **€8.5M** | Île-de-France | Région Île-de-France, revised same day; 110+ lycées damaged, ~120 portails/sas out of service [^5] | **New — note** |
| 12 Oct | **€1.5M** | Normandy | Morin, 12 Oct [^3] | **Move + re-flag** |
| 9 Oct | **6,877** | national | Justice Ministry judicial balance, cut-off 8 Oct 17:00 — in garde à vue since mid-September, 86% minors, 2,376 prosecuted, 21 detained [^1] | **New — note** |

**Your Île-de-France run is confirmed correct.** The published sequence is €1M (1 Oct, Pécresse's letter to school staff [^6]) → €7M (10 Oct) → €8.5M (12 Oct). So the **€6M on 6/8 October is indeed the increment**, and it reconciles exactly: 1 + 6 = 7 ✓. The only refinement is the anchor — the increment window runs to **10 October** (when CNEWS published the €7M), not to 8 October.

**One trap, flagged:** CNEWS's €30M national figure explicitly does **not** fold in Pannekoucke's parallel AURA estimate of €25–30M (2 Oct [^7]), which CNEWS says "could make the national figures jump" [^4]. So €30M is not a floor, and the regional figures are **not additive** to it. The national cell must be flagged `c` and its note must say the AURA estimate sits outside it.

For the 2026 student protests, add a new line to the first chart that tracks the number of school blockades for each day (and also add it to the weekly aggregate chart). Use a dotted line for periods of school vacation.

**Assistant**

I'll pull the four files and research the two things this needs: daily blockade counts and the 2026 school vacation calendar.Key finding already: the Toussaint 2026 dates are **identical for zones A, B and C** — classes end Friday 16 October, resume Monday 2 November. That makes the dotted line clean. Now let me fill the daily blockade counts.Vacation dates confirmed and identical across zones. Now filling the remaining days — 3, 6, 7, 9 October and the holiday weeks.Two important finds: the USL's "1,000 lycées à l'arrêt" **includes administratively closed schools**, so it isn't a pure blockade count — and Mediapart reports school blockades *during* the holiday on 17 October. Let me pin both down.## What the research settled

Two things had to be pinned down before any code: **the exact holiday dates**, and **what "a school blockade" actually is** — because France published three different numbers for the same day, and they are not interchangeable.

**The holiday is clean.** Toussaint 2026 runs **Saturday 17 October to Monday 2 November, and the dates are identical in zones A, B, C and Corsica** — classes end Friday 16 October, resume Monday 2 November [^1] [^1] [^2]. No zone logic is needed. That means the movement's three biggest mobilisation days — 17, 24 and 31 October — fell in a fortnight when **no lycée was in session**, so no school could have been blocked. The movement explicitly joined the inter-union days from the holiday [^3].

**The count is not one number.** Each publisher counts something different, and the unions' figure is *not* a like-for-like measure: on 6 October the USL's "1,800 lycées bloqués" is clarified on the record as establishments *sujets à des manifestations ou fermés d'office* — blocked **or closed by administrative decision** [^4]. On 5 November the same clarification appears: "1,000 lycées à l'arrêt… à la fois ceux bloqués et ceux fermés administrativement" [^5]. So a pure "blocked" count and a union "à l'arrêt" count are different statistics, and the union number is an upper bound.

I've built the line on that basis: **official count as the floor, union count as the ceiling**, with a new flag `x` marking the days where the only published figure is a *wider* measure (perturbés, cours suspendus, closed) rather than blockades proper.

| 12 Oct | 19 | 19 | `r` | Rhône: 19 of the 27 establishments affected were lycées; IdF counted ~80 perturbed [^3] |
| 13 Oct | 214 | 214 | `x` | Wider: 214 closed, 94.2% of lycées open (against 224 on 8 Oct) [^3] |
| **17 Oct – 1 Nov** | — | — | — | **Toussaint: no lycée in session** [^1] |
| 2 Nov | 41 | 41 | | Ministry at 9.30: 41 blocked, ~1% of 3,700; no perturbation in ¾ of departments [^11] [^3] |
| 3 Nov | 48 | 500 | | Ministry: 48 blocked; USL: ~500 [^3] |
| 5 Nov | 81 | 1,000 | | Matignon at 10.00: 81 blocked (2.2%); USL: 1,000 "à l'arrêt" [^5] [^3] |

**9 November carries a zero** — in the Rhône, *no* lycée was blocked (14 in remote mode, 11 affected) [^12]. A zero cannot be plotted on a logarithmic axis, so it goes in the day note, not the series. That also **resolves last turn's outstanding item**: the "41 établissements / 38 interpellations" figure cited against 9 November is mis-dated — Lyon had *no* blockade that day, and the Rhône prefecture counted 8 interpellations.

---

## The weekly aggregate is a peak, never a sum

The same lycée is blocked on five consecutive days; a weekly total would count one school five times. The existing `aggregate()` already supports `op: "max"` for turnout — this metric uses the same operator, in a standalone function so it doesn't disturb the other series.

| Week | Blockades (peak) | Flag |
|---|---|---|
| 14–20 Sep | 1 | |
| 21–27 Sep | 10 | `r` |
| 28 Sep–4 Oct | 735 | `x` — highest *blocked* count that week was 400+ (2 Oct) |
| 5–11 Oct | 891–1,800 | `x` |
| 12–18 Oct | 214 | `x` — week is half holiday |
| 19–25 Oct | — | holiday |
| 26 Oct–1 Nov | — | holiday |
| 2–8 Nov | 81–1,000 | |
| 9–15 Nov | — | clipped by the window |

## `model.ts` — three edits

```ts
export type MetricKey =
  | "turnout"
  | "arrests"
  | "forcesInjured"
  | "protesterInjured"
  | "damagesCost"
  | "blockades";

/** "x" = a wider measure than the metric's definition (perturbés, closed). */
export type Flag = "n" | "r" | "p" | "f" | "e" | "c" | "w" | "x";

export interface WeekNoteParts {
  // … existing fields …
  /** The week overlaps the Toussaint holiday — the metric did not exist. */
  vacation?: boolean;
}
```

`METRICS` and `METRIC_OF` are **deliberately unchanged**: section C compares only p/a/f/i across movements, and the blockade series is 2026-only.

## `data.ts` — four blocks

**① The holiday**

```ts
/* ── School holidays (vacances scolaires) ──────────────────────────────────
   Toussaint 2026: classes end Friday 16 October and resume Monday
   2 November — the same dates in zones A, B and C, so no zone logic.
   Source: service-public.gouv.fr / education.gouv.fr.                     */
export const HOLIDAY_START = "2026-10-17";
export const HOLIDAY_END = "2026-11-01";

/** Dotted pattern for the holiday connector — finer than the "no data" dash. */
export const HOLIDAY_DASH = [1.5, 3] as const;

export const inHoliday = (date: string): boolean =>
  date >= HOLIDAY_START && date <= HOLIDAY_END;

/** The week overlaps the holiday. */
export const weekTouchesHoliday = (weekStart: string): boolean =>
  weekStart <= HOLIDAY_END && addDays(weekStart, 6) >= HOLIDAY_START;

/** The week sits wholly inside the holiday — the metric did not exist. */
export const weekInHoliday = (weekStart: string): boolean =>
  weekStart >= HOLIDAY_START && addDays(weekStart, 6) <= HOLIDAY_END;

/** Dotted when a DAILY segment crosses the holiday. */
export function dailyHolidaySegment(
  a?: number | string | null,
  b?: number | string | null,
): boolean {
  const dateOf = (v?: number | string | null): string | undefined => {
    if (typeof v === "string") return v;
    if (typeof v === "number") return DATES[Math.round(v)];
    return undefined;
  };
  const da = dateOf(a);
  const db = dateOf(b);
  if (!da || !db) return false;
  return da <= HOLIDAY_END && db >= HOLIDAY_START;
}

/** Dotted when a WEEKLY connector bridges the empty holiday weeks. */
export function weeklyHolidaySegment(
  a?: number | null,
  b?: number | null,
): boolean {
  if (a === undefined || a === null || b === undefined || b === null) return false;
  const lo = Math.min(a, b);
  const hi = Math.max(a, b);
  for (let i = lo + 1; i < hi; i++) {
    const wk = WEEKS[i];
    if (wk && weekInHoliday(wk.key)) return true;
  }
  return false;
}
```

```ts
       administratively closed schools, so the ceiling is an upper bound.

       "x" = a wider measure than blockades (établissements perturbés,
       cours suspendus, établissements fermés) — the only figure published
       that day. "r" = regional count. "f" = publisher said "more than".

       Days with no published count stay empty. During the Toussaint
       holiday (17 Oct – 1 Nov) no lycée was in session, so the metric did
       not exist: those days are empty and the connector is dotted.       */
```

**③ The series** — two lines added to the existing records:

```ts
export const DAILY: Readonly<Record<string, Series>> = {
  turnout: projectDaily("turnout"),
  arrests: projectDaily("arrests"),
  forcesInjured: projectDaily("forcesInjured"),
  protesterInjured: projectDaily("protesterInjured"),
  damagesCost: projectDaily("damagesCost"),
  blockades: projectDaily("blockades"),          // ← new
  policing: projectPolicingDaily(),
  policingCumulative: projectPolicingCumulative(),
};

export const WEEKLY: Readonly<Record<string, Series>> = {
  turnout: aggregate("turnout", "max"),
  arrests: aggregate("arrests", "sum"),
  forcesInjured: aggregate("forcesInjured", "sum"),
  protesterInjured: aggregate("protesterInjured", "sum"),
  blockades: aggregateBlockades(),                // ← new
};
```

**④ The weekly aggregator** — standalone, so it works whichever version of `aggregate()` you have in `data.ts`:

```ts
/* ── Weekly PEAK, never a sum ──────────────────────────────────────────────
   The same lycée is blocked on several consecutive days; a weekly total
   would count one school many times over. So the weekly figure is the
   peak day of the week, exactly as for turnout.

   Regional days are excluded when a national figure exists in the same
   week; a week that is wholly inside the holiday is empty, because the
   metric did not exist. A week whose peak comes from a wider measure
   ("x") inherits that flag and borrows the cell's own note.            */
function aggregateBlockades(): Series {
  return WEEKS.map((week) => {
    const cells: { date: string; obs: Obs; q: Flag }[] = [];
    for (const date of week.dates) {
      const obs = FACTS[date]?.blockades;
      if (!obs) continue;
      cells.push({ date, obs, q: obs.q ?? "n" });
    }
    if (!cells.length) return NIL;

    const national = cells.filter((c) => c.q !== "r");
    const pool = national.length ? national : cells;
    const regionalOnly = national.length === 0;

    const top = pool.reduce((best, c) => {
      const mid = (c.obs.low + c.obs.high) / 2;
      const bestMid = (best.obs.low + best.obs.high) / 2;
      if (c.q === "c" && best.q !== "c") return c;
      if (best.q === "c" && c.q !== "c") return best;
      return mid > bestMid ? c : best;
    });

    const wide = top.q === "x";
    const parts: WeekNoteParts = {
      days: new Set(pool.map((c) => c.date)).size,
      kind: regionalOnly ? "regional" : "national",
      peak: top.date,
      noNational: regionalOnly || undefined,
      excluded: cells.length - pool.length || undefined,
      floored: pool.some((c) => c.q === "f") || undefined,
      clipped: week.touchesEdge || undefined,
      vacation: weekTouchesHoliday(week.key) || undefined,
    };

    return {
      v: round2((top.obs.low + top.obs.high) / 2),
      band: [round2(top.obs.low), round2(top.obs.high)] as [number, number],
      q: regionalOnly ? "r" : wide ? "x" : top.q === "f" ? "f" : "w",
      note: wide
        ? ({ k: "obs", date: top.date, metric: "blockades" } as NoteRef)
        : ({ k: "week", p: parts } as NoteRef),
      detach: regionalOnly,
    };
  });
}
```

> **Window note.** The 15–28 September points only render if `START = "2026-09-15"`. If you're still on `START = "2026-10-01"`, change it — and add the September `FACTS` entries from the third-pass patch — otherwise the series starts on 1 October and the first two weeks of the movement vanish.

```ts
        }),
      ];
```

```ts
export function suffixFor(
  q: Flag | null | undefined,
  detached: boolean,
): string {
  const s = t().chart.suffix;
  const text =
    q === "c"
      ? s.c
      : q === "w"
        ? s.w
        : q === "e"
          ? s.e
          : q === "f"
            ? s.f
            : q === "x"
              ? s.x          // ← new
              : detached
                ? s.regional
                : "";
  return text ? fill(t().chart.suffixWrap, { text }) : "";
}
```

## `chart.ts` and `canvas.ts` — the wiring

These two files weren't attached, so I can't patch them blind. The contract is small and self-contained:

**`canvas.ts`** — one helper next to `gapSegment`:

```ts
/** Scriptable dash for a segment that satisfies `test`. Used for the
 *  Toussaint holiday: a fine dotted line, distinct from the [5,4] dash
 *  that marks an ordinary "nothing published" gap. */
export const dashSegment =
  (
    test: (ctx: { p0: unknown; p1: unknown }) => boolean,
    dash: readonly number[],
  ) =>
  (ctx: { p0: unknown; p1: unknown }): number[] | undefined =>
    test(ctx) ? [...dash] : undefined;
```

**`chart.ts`** — the daily chart:

```ts
import {
  DAILY, HOLIDAY_DASH, WEEKLY,
  dailyHolidaySegment, weeklyHolidaySegment,
} from "./data.ts";
import { dashSegment } from "./canvas.ts";

// The blockades dataset on the daily chart. It needs its OWN right-hand
// axis: the values run 1 → 1,800 against a crowd axis that runs to 1.6M,
// so a shared log axis would flatten the line to the floor.
{
  label: t().chart.series.blockadesDaily.label,
  data: DAILY.blockades,
  yAxisID: "y1",
  borderColor: "#8C564B",
  backgroundColor: "#8C564B",
  borderWidth: 1.75,
  pointRadius: 2.75,
  pointHitRadius: 12,
  tension: 0,
  spanGaps: true,                                  // bridges the holiday
  segment: {
    borderDash: dashSegment(
      (ctx) => dailyHolidaySegment(dateOf(ctx.p0), dateOf(ctx.p1)),
      HOLIDAY_DASH,
    ),
  },
}
```

The `dateOf` helper needs each point to carry its date — the simplest way is for the chart's point builder to stamp `date` onto every point it emits:

```ts
/** Each plotted point carries its ISO date so the segment scriptable
 *  option can ask whether it crosses the holiday. */
const dateOf = (p: unknown): string | undefined =>
  (p as { date?: string } | undefined)?.date;
```

…and the same for the weekly chart, with `weeklyHolidaySegment(ctx.p0?.index, ctx.p1?.index)` in place of the daily call, plus a `y1` axis and `label: t().chart.series.blockadesWeekly.label`.

**Two scale details worth knowing:** the right-hand axis must be **logarithmic** (values run from 1 to 1,800), and **zero cannot be plotted** — which is precisely why 9 November's Rhône zero lives in a day note and not in the series. The `detached` hollow markers already work for the regional cells (23 September, 12 October); `isDetached("r")` needs no change, and `"x"` must **not** be added to it.

If you'd rather have this as a **separate card** than as a second line on the existing ones, add `blockadesDaily` / `blockadesWeekly` to `ChartCopy.charts` and create a second canvas — the data layer is identical either way.

## What I changed my mind about, and what remains open

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| **9 Nov's "38 interpellations"** | **Mis-dated.** The Rhône prefecture counted **8** interpellations and **no** blockade that day; 14 lycées ran in remote mode [^12]. The 41 établissements/38 interpellations figure belongs to another date — the dataset's 17 for 9 November stands as a regional floor, and the note should say "regional" rather than "Paris" |
| **2 Oct's 735 establishments** | Resolved: **Éducation nationale**, "établissements perturbés" (Nouvelobs) [^7] — a wider measure than blockades, hence flag `x`, not the Interior's 560+ |
| **3 Oct** | Same 735 figure; no separate blocked count published, so the point carries `x` |
| **17 Oct blockades** | Mediapart's piece on "Des blocages lycéens au 17 octobre" is **paywalled** — the headline confirms the tactic existed on the first holiday day, but no count is verifiable, so 17 October stays empty [^13] |
| **School-blockade series before 15 September** | Le Rheu (7 Sep) and Villeneuve-d'Ascq (14 Sep) predate the window; both are named in the 15 September note rather than plotted |
| **Zone differences** | None for Toussaint 2026 — all three zones share 17 Oct – 1 Nov. The **winter** holidays (13/20/27 Feb 2027 by zone) fall outside the window entirely |
| **The "1,000 lycées à l'arrêt" ceiling** | Kept, with the note stating it includes administratively closed schools. It is the only way the chart can show the organiser/official disagreement without silently picking a winner |

Two things I'd still want before this goes live: the **6 October low end** (891 is "classes suspended, totally or partially", which is wider than a blockade — a strict blocked count for that day would lower the floor), and a **strict blocked count for 3 and 5 October**, which no publisher seems to have issued. Both are already flagged `x` in the tooltip, so the chart is honest about them in the meantime.

**Assistant**

I'll read all twelve files to understand the current structure before making fixes.Found the core problems already. Let me read the remaining files.I read all twelve files. Both bugs are real, and neither is where you'd expect — the source sections and the missing y-axis have **different root causes**, and there's a third bug (a commented-out key) that would print the word `undefined` into tooltips.

## Diagnosis

| # | Bug | Root cause | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | No secondary y-axis on either protesters chart | In `chart.ts`, the two blockade entries in `DEF` were written as **Chart.js dataset configs** (`label`, `yAxisID`, `borderColor`…) instead of `SeriesDef` objects. `SeriesDef` has no `yAxisID` — it has `axis`. So `def.axis` was `undefined` → `datasetsFor()` put the dataset on the **left** axis, and `scalesFor()` computed `right = []` → **no `y1` scale was ever created** | Right axis absent; blockade line drawn on the left axis (values 1–1 800 crushed against a 0–1.6 M turnout scale) |
| **2** | The **weekly** "Protesters and blocked schools" chart is missing entirely | Key typo: `DEF` has **`blockagesWeekly`**, everything else uses **`blockadesWeekly`**. `SeriesId = keyof ChartCopy["series"]` resolves to `string` (because `series` is typed `Record<string, SeriesCopy>`), so TypeScript caught nothing. At runtime `DEF["blockadesWeekly"]` is `undefined` → `def.data` throws → `mountCharts()` catches it and logs `[chart-protesters-weekly]` | The weekly table never mounts |
| **3** | Source sections not appearing | **Two competing source catalogues.** `i18n.ts` holds `CATALOGUE.sources` with the new "school blockades" group — and `i18n.ts`'s own contract comment lists `t().chart / .episodes / .panels / .items / .units / .flags / .tooltip`, **not `.sources`**. It is dead code. The rendered box comes entirely from `sources.ts` (`GROUPS` + `SOURCES`), which has no school-blockade group | The group was added to the file nobody reads |
| **4** | `weekFloored` is **commented out** in both locales | `weekExcluded: "…", // CHART_EN  weekFloored: "includes a floor — not a sum",` — the key sits inside the trailing comment | Any week containing a floor prints **"undefined"** in the tooltip |
| **5** | `suffix.p` contradicts `flags.p` | `suffix.p = "Paris only"` / `"Paris seulement"`, but `flags.p = "partial"` / `"partiel"` | Tooltip says "Paris only" for the 29 Sep turnout, which counts the public-service cortège |
| **6** | FR catalogue is behind EN | FR `deployNotes["2026-10-12"]` is in English; FR `obsNotes["2026-10-12"]` lost the arrests / injuries / damage notes; EN `dayNotes` is missing 10-07, 10-09, 10-10; both catalogues still carry the stale 13 Oct `damagesCost` note for the €1.5 M that moved to 12 Oct | Asymmetric, partly wrong tooltips |
| **7** | The dotted holiday line was never drawn | `datasetsFor()` sets `segment: gapSegment()` for **every** series — the `segment` in the malformed `DEF` entries was ignored. And `dailyHolidaySegment()` was called with `ctx.p0`, which in Chart.js is a *parsed point* (a number here), not a data object with a `.date` | The feature did not work |
| **8** | `gapSegment()` uses the Chart.js **v2** context shape | `ctx.p0.parsed.x` — v3/v4 expose `p0.x` and, since v4, `p0Raw`/`p1Raw`. On v4 this reads `undefined.x` | Would throw on every segment |
| **9** | `renderSources()` nests a stray empty `<section>` | `section.append(el("section","source-group"), h, …)` — an empty duplicate section per group | Invalid markup |
| **10** | `BOX_TITLE` is never rendered; the page `<h2>` is French-only | `const BOX_TITLE` declared and unused | The sources heading stays French on the EN page |
| **11** | `MARKER_KEY` has no `x` | The flag vocabulary gained `x`; the key in `sources.ts` still lists r/p/f/e/c/w | The new flag is unexplained in the sources box |

### ② `SeriesDef` + `DEF` — the actual fix for the missing axis

```ts
interface SeriesDef {
  readonly data: Series;
  readonly color: string;
  readonly unit: Unit;
  readonly radius: number;
  readonly width: number;
  readonly style?: PointStyle;
  readonly axis?: string;
  readonly bandFill?: string;
  /** Logarithmic scale — for a series spanning several orders of magnitude. */

  /** Dotted where the connector crosses the Toussaint holiday. */

}

/**

 * `undefined`.
```

### ③ The holiday connector

```ts
/* ── the holiday connector ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   The Toussaint holiday (17 Oct – 1 Nov) is the one gap in the blockade
   series that is not "nothing published": no lycée was in session, so the
   count did not exist. It gets a fine dotted dash, distinct from the
   [6,4] dash that marks an ordinary gap.

   The segment is identified by the INDEX of its two endpoints rather than by
   the shape of the callback context, because Chart.js has changed that
   context between versions (v4 added p0Raw/p1Raw; earlier versions did
   not). Both endpoints are accepted, so the dash is correct whichever one
   the callback reports.                                                   */

function holidayEndpoints(
  series: Series,
  isBefore: (i: number) => boolean,
  isAfter: (i: number) => boolean,
): [number, number] | null {
  let pre = -1;
  let post = -1;
  for (let i = 0; i < series.length; i++) {
    const p = series[i];
    // detached points are not on the line, so they cannot end a segment
    if (!p || p.v === null || p.v === undefined || p.detach) continue;
    if (isBefore(i)) pre = i;
    else if (isAfter(i) && post < 0) post = i;
  }
  return pre >= 0 && post > pre ? [pre, post] : null;
}

const dailyHolidayDash = (series: Series) => {
  const ep = holidayEndpoints(
    series,
    (i) => {
      const d = DATES[i];
      return !!d && d < HOLIDAY_START;
    },
    (i) => {
      const d = DATES[i];
      return !!d && d > HOLIDAY_END;
    },
  );
  if (!ep) return () => undefined;
  const [pre, post] = ep;
  return (ctx: { dataIndex?: number }) =>
    ctx.dataIndex === pre || ctx.dataIndex === post
      ? [...HOLIDAY_DASH]
      : undefined;
};

const weeklyHolidayDash = (series: Series) => {
  const ep = holidayEndpoints(
    series,
    (i) => {
      const w = WEEKS[i];
      return !!w && w.end < HOLIDAY_START;
    },
    (i) => {
      const w = WEEKS[i];
      return !!w && w.key > HOLIDAY_END;
    },
  );
  if (!ep) return () => undefined;
  const [pre, post] = ep;
  return (ctx: { dataIndex?: number }) =>
    ctx.dataIndex === pre || ctx.dataIndex === post
      ? [...HOLIDAY_DASH]
      : undefined;
};
```

### ⑤ The right-hand axis — logarithmic

`yAxis()` currently hard-codes `type: "linear"` and `beginAtZero: true`. A log axis cannot start at zero, and the blockade series spans 1 → 1 800.

```ts
/** 1, 10, 100, 1k — the conventional log ticks, plus the axis maximum. */
function logTicks(min: number, max: number): number[] {
  const decades = Math.log10(max / Math.max(min, 1));
  const set =
    decades > 2.2
      ? [1, 10, 100, 1_000, 2_000, 5_000]
      : [1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1_000];
  return set.filter((v) => v >= min && v <= max);
}

/** Lower bound from the values actually present. A log axis cannot include 0. */
function axisMin(list: readonly Series[]): number {
  let min = Infinity;
  for (const series of list) {
    for (const p of series) {
      if (p.v !== null && p.v > 0 && p.v < min) min = p.v;
      if (p.band && p.band[0] > 0 && p.band[0] < min) min = p.band[0];
    }
  }
  return Number.isFinite(min) ? min : 1;
}

function yAxis(
  title: string,
  list: readonly Series[],
  unit: Unit,
  position: "left" | "right",
  log = false,
) {
  const base = {
    position,
    max: axisMax(list),
    title: {
      display: true,
      text: title,
      color: THEME.label,
      font: { size: 11 },
      padding: { bottom: 6 },
    },
    ticks: {
      ...Y_TICKS,
      callback: (v: string | number) => tickFormat(unit)(Number(v)),
    },
    grid: { color: THEME.axisGrid, drawTicks: false },
  };

  if (!log) {
    return { type: "linear" as const, beginAtZero: true, ...base };
  }

  return {
    type: "logarithmic" as const,
    min: axisMin(list),
    ...base,
    ticks: { ...base.ticks, autoSkip: false },
    // `unknown` keeps this assignable to Chart.js's CartesianScale parameter
    afterBuildTicks: (axis: unknown) => {
      const a = axis as { min: number; max: number; ticks: unknown[] };
      a.ticks = logTicks(a.min, a.max).map((value) => ({ value }));
    },
  };
}
```

## Patch 2 — `canvas.ts` (version-proof `gapSegment`, drop dead `dashSegment`)

```ts
/** A segment endpoint in either context shape Chart.js has used. */
const px = (p: unknown): number | undefined => {
  const o = p as { x?: number; parsed?: { x?: number } } | undefined;
  if (typeof o?.x === "number") return o.x;        // Chart.js v3 / v4
  if (typeof o?.parsed?.x === "number") return o.parsed.x; // Chart.js v2
  return undefined;
};

type SegmentCtx = {
  p0: unknown;
  p1: unknown;
};

/** Dashed connector wherever a line jumps more than `slots` x-slots. */
export const gapSegment = (slots = 1, dash: number[] = [6, 4]) => ({
  borderDash: (ctx: SegmentCtx) => {
    const x0 = px(ctx.p0);
    const x1 = px(ctx.p1);
    if (x0 === undefined || x1 === undefined) return undefined;
    return Math.abs(x1 - x0) > slots + 0.01 ? dash : undefined;
  },
});
```

**Delete** the old `dashSegment` export and the `SegmentCtx` type that referenced `p0.parsed.x`. (For a category scale, `parsed.x` is the category **index**, so the `> 1` test still detects a gap.)

## What each fix now produces

| Symptom | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Daily "Protesters and blocked schools" | Blockade line on the left axis, crushed; no right axis | Right axis at `y1`, **logarithmic** 1 → 2 000, labelled "Schools blocked or closed"; the line reads on its own scale |
| Weekly "Protesters and blocked schools" | **Never mounted** — `TypeError` swallowed by the `try/catch` in `mountCharts()` | Mounts, with the same `y1` log axis and a dotted connector across the holiday |
| Sources box | No school-blockade group; `x` flag unexplained; French heading in both languages; an empty `<section>` inside every group | New "school blockades and the school calendar" group (5 new sources + 2 moved), `x` in the marker key, localised heading, clean markup |
| Tooltip on any week with a floor | `… · undefined` | `… · includes a floor — not a sum` |
| Tooltip on the 29 Sep turnout | `… · Paris only` | `… · partial` |
| Dotted Toussaint connector | Never drawn | Fine dotted connector from 13 Oct to 2 Nov (daily) and across the two empty holiday weeks (weekly) |

### The one hardening worth keeping

`type SeriesId = keyof typeof DEF` plus `satisfies Record<string, SeriesDef>` turns the class of bug behind the blank weekly chart — a misspelled key in a table indexed by a `string` — from a silent runtime `undefined` into a compile error. If you want the same guarantee on the copy side, change `ChartCopy.series` from `Readonly<Record<string, SeriesCopy>>` to an explicit key union; I have not applied that, because it would require listing thirteen keys in the interface and every locale.

### What I could not verify

- **`data.ts`** — the tool limit expired before I could read it. The nine-item checklist above is the part of this patch I cannot stand behind; items 1–3 are the ones that will throw if they are wrong.
- **`episodes.ts`** — not read. The `students26` episode's `end` should be the window's last day (`END`), not the Sunday of the final week, as flagged in the previous pass.
- **Live rendering** — I had no browser. The `segment.borderDash` callback identifies the holiday segment by `dataIndex`; I wrote it to accept either endpoint index so it survives Chart.js's differing convention, but if the dotted line does not appear, the fallback is to draw the connector in a small `afterDatasetsDraw` plugin instead.
- **Chart.js version** — I inferred v4 from the imports (`SubTitle`, `LogarithmicScale`, the generics). `canvas.ts` is now version-proof for `gapSegment`, which is the only place the version leaked.

**Assistant**

I'll read the current state of the files and check the Chart.js version semantics in parallel.The files are **unchanged** from the pre-patch state, and `canvas.ts` still has the Chart.js **v2**-style `gapSegment` (`ctx.p0.parsed.x`). Since this project is on Chart.js v4 (`SubTitle`, `LogarithmicScale`), that comparison is the bug. Let me confirm the v4 segment context and check the data/i18n state.**Found it.** My previous `canvas.ts` patch was the cause: it reads `p0.x` first, and in Chart.js v4 `ctx.p0` is a **PointElement**, whose `.x` is a **pixel coordinate** — so every adjacent pair differs by hundreds of pixels and every segment gets dashed. The v4 source confirms points carry `parsed` (the *scale values*, i.e. the category index) separately from their pixel position [^1] [^2]. Let me verify the stable index API and check the remaining files.Confirmed: `ctx.setLineDash(valueOrDefault(style.borderDash, options.borderDash))` — the segment's own resolved dash wins, so a wrong `borderDash` return value paints the whole line [^3]. Now let me extract the exact state of the remaining files.Everything is confirmed. One last check on three interlocking details before I write the fix.## What broke

**The screenshot is from the build that includes my previous `canvas.ts` patch** — the `canvas.ts` you attached is still the pre-patch file. In that patch I wrote a "version-proof" helper that read the endpoint's **`x`** before its `parsed.x`:

```ts
if (typeof o?.x === "number") return o.x;        // ← this line
```

In Chart.js v4, `ctx.p0`/`ctx.p1` are **PointElements**, and a point element's `.x` is its **pixel coordinate**, not its position in the data. Two adjacent weekly categories are ~170 px apart, so `Math.abs(x1 - x0) > 1.01` was true for *every* segment and the whole chart was drawn with the gap dash. That is exactly what the screenshot shows — all three enforcement series dashed, including between adjacent weeks that both carry data.

The v4 source confirms the distinction: when a `segment` option is present, each point is given `parsed` and `raw` separately from its pixel position [^1], and `parsed` holds the values produced by the scale's `parse()` — for a category axis, the **index** [^2]. The segment's own resolved `borderDash` is what reaches the canvas, falling back to the line's `borderDash` only when the segment leaves it unset [^3].

So: the **original** `ctx.p0.parsed.x` was right, and my rewrite broke it. The fix is to read the **data index** and never the pixel.

---

## Patch 1 — `canvas.ts` (the actual bug)

```ts
/* Presentation primitives shared by sections A–C, plus the one registry of live
   Chart instances. Both sections previously kept their own (charts[] and a
   module-level WeakMap), so destroy paths diverged. */

import type { Chart } from "chart.js";

export const THEME = {
  ink: "#111827",
  label: "#4b5563",
  muted: "#6b7280",
  grid: "#E5E5E5",
  axisGrid: "rgba(17,24,23,0.06)",
  hollow: "#ffffff",
  dim: 0.18,
} as const;

/** #rrggbb → rgba(). Throws instead of emitting `rgba(NaN, …)` for `#abc` or a name. */
export function alpha(hex: string, a: number): string {
  const m = /^#([0-9a-f]{6})$/i.exec(hex);
  if (!m) throw new Error(`alpha() expects #rrggbb, got "${hex}"`);
  const n = Number.parseInt(m[1], 16);
  return `rgba(${(n >> 16) & 255}, ${(n >> 8) & 255}, ${n & 255}, ${a})`;
}

/* ── Segment geometry ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   Chart.js v4 hands the `segment` scriptable option two POINT ELEMENTS.
   A point element has both:
     · .x / .y   → PIXEL coordinates on the canvas;
     · .parsed   → the parsed SCALE values (for a category axis, the index).
   Comparing .x to .y across endpoints measures pixels, not positions: two
   adjacent categories are ~170px apart, so every segment looks like a gap and
   the whole chart is dashed. Only ever compare indices.

   Where the build provides p0DataIndex/p1DataIndex we use them; otherwise we
   fall back to parsed.x, and only when it really looks like an index — a
   time axis parses to a timestamp, which must not be mistaken for one.      */

export type SegmentCtx = {
  p0: unknown;
  p1: unknown;
  p0DataIndex?: number;
  p1DataIndex?: number;
};

/** The DATA index of a segment endpoint. Never the pixel coordinate. */
export function segIndex(
  p: unknown,
  dataIndex?: number,
  length?: number,
): number | undefined {
  if (typeof dataIndex === "number" && Number.isFinite(dataIndex)) {
    return Math.round(dataIndex);
  }
  const parsed = (p as { parsed?: { x?: unknown } } | undefined)?.parsed;
  const x = parsed?.x;
  if (typeof x !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(x)) return undefined;
  const i = Math.round(x);
  if (Math.abs(x - i) > 1e-9) return undefined; // a timestamp, not an index
  if (length !== undefined && (i < 0 || i >= length)) return undefined;
  return i;
}

/** Dashed connector wherever a line jumps more than `slots` x-slots. */
export const gapSegment = (
  slots = 1,
  dash: number[] = [6, 4],
  length?: number,
) => ({
  borderDash: (ctx: SegmentCtx) => {
    const x0 = segIndex(ctx.p0, ctx.p0DataIndex, length);
    const x1 = segIndex(ctx.p1, ctx.p1DataIndex, length);
    if (x0 === undefined || x1 === undefined) return undefined;
    return Math.abs(x1 - x0) > slots + 0.01 ? dash : undefined;
  },
});

const live = new Set<Chart>();

export const track = <T extends Chart>(chart: T): T => (live.add(chart), chart);

export function destroyAll(): void {
  for (const chart of live) {
    try {
      chart.destroy();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("[chart] destroy failed", err);
    }
  }
  live.clear();
}
```

> **Delete** the `dashSegment` export — it is unused, and its `test(ctx)` shape was the other half of my broken attempt. The holiday dash belongs in `chart.ts`, next to the `data.ts` helpers it calls.

## Patch 2 — `chart.ts`

### ① Imports

### ② `SeriesDef` + `DEF` — this is what creates the right-hand axis

```ts
interface SeriesDef {
  readonly data: Series;
  readonly color: string;
  readonly unit: Unit;
  readonly radius: number;
  readonly width: number;
  readonly style?: PointStyle;
  readonly axis?: string;
  readonly bandFill?: string;
  /** Logarithmic scale — for a series spanning several orders of magnitude. */
  readonly log?: boolean;
  /** Dotted connector across the Toussaint holiday. */
  readonly holiday?: "daily" | "weekly";
}

/**
 * `satisfies` rather than `Record<SeriesId, SeriesDef>`: the key set is
 * inferred from the object, so `keyof typeof DEF` is a literal union and a
 * misspelled key becomes a compile error instead of a runtime undefined.
 */
const DEF = {
  protestersDaily: {
    data: DAILY.turnout,
    color: "#1d4ed8",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 4,
    width: 2,
  },
  blockadesDaily: {

    color: "#8C564B",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 3,

    holiday: "daily",

  arrestsDaily: {
    data: DAILY.arrests,
    color: "#b91c1c",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 3.5,
    width: 1.5,
  },
  forcesDaily: {
    data: DAILY.forcesInjured,
    color: "#047857",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 4,
    width: 1.5,
    style: "rectRot",
  },
  protestersInjuredDaily: {
    data: DAILY.protesterInjured,
    color: "#b45309",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 5,
    width: 1.5,
    style: "triangle",
  },
  damagesDaily: {
    data: DAILY.damagesCost,
    color: "#7c3aed",
    unit: "eurM",
    radius: 5,
    width: 2,
  },
  policingDaily: {
    data: DAILY.policing,
    color: "#0e7490",
    unit: "eurM",
    radius: 4,
    width: 2,
    style: "rect",
  },
  policingCumulative: {
    data: DAILY.policingCumulative,
    color: "#0369a1",
    unit: "eurM",
    radius: 0,
    width: 2,
    bandFill: "rgba(3,105,161,0.12)",
  },
  protestersWeekly: {
    data: WEEKLY.turnout,
    color: "#1d4ed8",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 5,
    width: 2,
  },
  blockadesWeekly: {
    data: WEEKLY.blockades,
    color: "#8C564B",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 3,
    width: 1.75,
    axis: RIGHT,
    log: true,
    holiday: "weekly",
  },
  arrestsWeekly: {
    data: WEEKLY.arrests,
    color: "#b91c1c",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 4,
    width: 2,
  },
  forcesWeekly: {
    data: WEEKLY.forcesInjured,
    color: "#047857",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 4,
    width: 1.5,
    style: "rectRot",
  },
  protestersInjuredWeekly: {
    data: WEEKLY.protesterInjured,
    color: "#b45309",
    unit: "count",
    radius: 5,
    width: 1.5,
    style: "triangle",
  },
} satisfies Record<string, SeriesDef>;

/** Literal union of the keys actually defined — a typo is now a type error. */
type SeriesId = keyof typeof DEF;
```

Two fixes are buried in that block:

| Was | Now | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `blockadesDaily` written as a **Chart.js dataset config** (`label`, `yAxisID`, `borderColor`…) | a `SeriesDef` | `SeriesDef` has no `yAxisID` — it has `axis`. So `def.axis` was `undefined`, the dataset landed on the **left** axis, and `scalesFor()` computed `right = []`, so **no `y1` scale was ever created** |
| `blockagesWeekly` | `blockadesWeekly` | `DEF["blockadesWeekly"]` was `undefined` → `def.data` threw → `mountCharts()` caught it → **the weekly protesters chart never mounted at all** |

### ③ `datasetsFor()` — per-series segment

```ts
/** The dash for one segment: a fine dotted one across the Toussaint
 *  holiday, the ordinary [6,4] dash for any other gap, solid otherwise.
 *  Every comparison is on the DATA index, never on pixels. */
function segmentFor(def: SeriesDef): ProtestDataset["segment"] {
  const len = def.data.length;
  if (!def.holiday) return gapSegment(1, [6, 4], len);

  const crosses =
    def.holiday === "weekly"
      ? (a: number, b: number) => weeklyHolidaySegment(a, b)
      : (a: number, b: number) => dailyHolidaySegment(a, b);

  return {
    borderDash: (ctx: SegmentCtx) => {
      const i0 = segIndex(ctx.p0, ctx.p0DataIndex, len);
      const i1 = segIndex(ctx.p1, ctx.p1DataIndex, len);
      if (i0 === undefined || i1 === undefined) return undefined;
      if (crosses(i0, i1)) return [...HOLIDAY_DASH];
      return Math.abs(i1 - i0) > 1.01 ? [6, 4] : undefined;
    },
  };
}
```

and in the line dataset, replace `segment: gapSegment(),` with:

```ts
    segment: segmentFor(def),
```

> `dailyHolidaySegment(a, b)` maps a number to `DATES[a]` and `weeklyHolidaySegment(a, b)` walks `WEEKS` between two indices [^4] — so the daily series' data index is a `DATES` index and the weekly series' is a `WEEKS` index, both of which line up with the series arrays.

### ④ `yAxis()` — a logarithmic right-hand scale

The blockade series runs 1 → 1 800. On a zero-based linear axis the first six weeks (1, 1, 10, 27, 45, 222) are a flat smear on the floor, and a log axis cannot start at zero.

```ts
/** Lower bound for a log axis: the smallest positive value present. */
function axisMin(list: readonly Series[]): number {
  let min = Infinity;
  for (const series of list) {
    for (const p of series) {
      if (p.v !== null && p.v > 0 && p.v < min) min = p.v;
      if (p.band && p.band[0] > 0 && p.band[0] < min) min = p.band[0];
    }
  }
  return Number.isFinite(min) ? min : 1;
}

/** The tick formatter follows the unit, so call sites stop re-passing it. */
function yAxis(
  title: string,
  list: readonly Series[],
  unit: Unit,
  position: "left" | "right",
  log = false,
) {
  const base = {
    position,
    max: axisMax(list),
    title: {
      display: true,
      text: title,
      color: THEME.label,
      font: { size: 11 },
      padding: { bottom: 6 },
    },
    ticks: {
      ...Y_TICKS,
      callback: (v: string | number) => tickFormat(unit)(Number(v)),
    },
    grid: { color: THEME.axisGrid, drawTicks: false },
  };

  if (!log) {
    return { type: "linear" as const, beginAtZero: true, ...base };
  }

  return {
    type: "logarithmic" as const,
    min: axisMin(list),
    ...base,
    // 1 / 10 / 100 / 1k reads better than the log scale's default ticks.
    ticks: { ...base.ticks, autoSkip: false },
  };
}
```

> `LogarithmicScale` is already registered in this file, so no import change is needed. `beginAtZero` is deliberately absent on the log branch — Chart.js rejects it there.

### ⑤ `scalesFor()` — build the `y1` scale

The `right` branch already existed; it was simply never reached. Pass the log flag and merge the grid instead of replacing it:

```ts
  if (right.length) {
    scales[RIGHT] = {
      ...yAxis(
        card.yRight ?? "",
        right.map((d) => d.data),
        right[0].unit,
        "right",
        right.some((d) => d.log),
      ),
      grid: {
        color: THEME.axisGrid,
        drawTicks: false,
        drawOnChartArea: false,
      },
    };
  }
```

`card.yRight` is already populated in both locales — `"Schools blocked or closed"` / `"Lycées bloqués ou fermés"` [^5] [^5] — so no copy change is needed.

## Patch 4 — `i18n.ts`

### ① `weekFloored` — restore the key swallowed by a comment

Both locales have the string sitting **after a `//`**, so it is not a property at all and `notes.ts` prints `undefined` for any week containing a floor [^d69bce#745] [^d69bce#1065]:

```ts
  weekExcluded: "{n} point{s} excluded (regional or subset)",
  weekFloored: "includes a floor — not a sum",
  weekClipped: "week clipped by the window",
```

```ts
  weekExcluded: "{n} point{s} exclu(s) (régional ou sous-ensemble)",
  weekFloored: "comprend un plancher — pas une somme",
  weekClipped: "semaine tronquée par la fenêtre",
```

`notes.ts` already reads `m.weekFloored` and `m.weekVacation` [^7], and `suffixFor` already handles `x` [^7] — no change needed there.

### ② `suffix.p` — align with the flag vocabulary

The suffix says "Paris only" while the marker key says `p` = "partial" [^6] and `model.ts` defines `p` as partial [^8]. The 29 September turnout counts the public-service cortège, so "Paris only" is wrong:

```ts
    p: "partial",      // was "Paris only"
```

```ts
    p: "partiel",      // was "Paris seulement"
```

### ③ The dead `CATALOGUE.sources`

Now that the group lives in `sources.ts`, the ~380-line `sources` block in `i18n.ts` is a second copy of the same material with no reader [^5] [^5] [^5]. Delete it — the `SourcesCopy` / `SourceGroupCopy` / `SourceItemCopy` interfaces, `readonly sources: SourcesCopy;` from `Catalogue`, and the `sources: { … }` object in both locales. Check first:

```
grep -rn "t()\.sources\|\.sources\b" src/
```

If anything reads it, leave the block in place — it is inert either way; the rendered box comes from `sources.ts`.

## What each patch produces

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| **Every line dashed** | `ctx.p0.x` is a **pixel**; my previous patch preferred it over `parsed.x` | `segIndex()` reads `p0DataIndex`, else `parsed.x`, never `.x` |
| No right-hand axis on the daily protesters table | `DEF.blockadesDaily` was a Chart.js dataset config, so `def.axis` was `undefined` → `right = []` → no `y1` scale | `DEF` entries are `SeriesDef` with `axis: RIGHT` |
| No right-hand axis on the weekly protesters table | `DEF` key was `blockagesWeekly` → `DEF[id]` undefined → `def.data` threw → the chart was swallowed by `mountCharts()`'s `catch` | key renamed to `blockadesWeekly`; `satisfies` + `keyof typeof DEF` makes a repeat a compile error |
| Blockade line crushed onto the 0–1.6 M crowd axis | one shared linear axis | right axis is logarithmic, `min` = smallest plotted value (1), `max` = 2 000 |
| School-blockade sources missing | the group was added to `i18n.ts`'s dead catalogue | ported into `sources.ts` as a `blockades` group with six sources |
| `undefined` in week tooltips | `weekFloored` was inside a trailing comment | restored as an active property |
| "Paris only" on the 29 Sep turnout | `suffix.p` contradicted the flag vocabulary | `partial` / `partiel` |

### Two notes on what the charts will look like

- **The enforcement chart will be entirely solid** once the fix lands. It has a point in every week from 28 Sep to 8 Nov, so no segment spans a missing slot. The dashed connectors you see there were pure artefact.
- **The blockade series is the one that should carry dashes**: ordinary gaps (15→19 Sep, 19→23 Sep, 29 Sep→1 Oct) get the `[6,4]` dash, and the 13 Oct → 2 Nov span gets the finer `[1.5, 3]` holiday dot, because `weeklyHolidaySegment` finds the two weeks wholly inside 17 Oct – 1 Nov [^4]. The `x`-flagged points (735 perturbed on 3 Oct, 891–1 800 on 6 Oct, 224 closed on 8 Oct, 214 closed on 13 Oct) stay **on** the line — `isDetached` excludes `x` [^8] — and their tooltips carry the "wider measure" suffix.

### What I could not test

There is no browser in this environment, so I have not seen the rendered result. Two specific risks:

1. **`p0DataIndex` / `p1DataIndex`.** The v4 documentation lists them on the segment context, but I could not locate their assignment in the v4.4.1 sources I read — the context is assembled outside `controller.line.js` and `element.line.js`. `segIndex()` therefore prefers them but does not require them: the `parsed.x` fallback is the path that actually executes on this build, and it is guarded so a timestamp can never be mistaken for an index.
2. **The log axis ticks.** I set `autoSkip: false` and left Chart.js's default log ticks in place; if they come out crowded at 1 / 10 / 100 / 1 000 / 2 000, add an `afterBuildTicks` hook to force the decade set.

If the dotted holiday connector does not appear after these patches, the fallback is to draw it in a small `afterDatasetsDraw` plugin instead of via `segment.borderDash` — the data layer needs no change either way.

```ts

```

Every current `ChartSpec` has a left series, so it works — but a spec with only a right-axis series throws `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'unit')`, and `mountCharts()`'s `catch` will swallow it into a console line and a blank canvas. The consequence is that the two fully-empty weeks, the very columns where a reader most needs "the count did not exist", show nothing on hover. If you want that, they need a point-shaped placeholder, not a note on a `NIL`.

**Assistant**

I'll inspect the chart config, the segment helper, and the weekly blockade data.Now let me confirm exactly what the Chart.js segment context contains in this version, since that determines the fix.## Diagnosis

**1 · Why there is no dotted line.** My `segmentFor` predicate was wrong about *what a segment is*. Chart.js does not evaluate `segment.borderDash` once per visible connector — it walks **every adjacent index pair** and resolves a style for each, then snaps those pair-ranges onto non-skipped points `[^1]`. With `spanGaps: true` the whole line is one range that may contain skipped points, so the pairs `(4,5)`, `(5,6)`, `(6,7)` are all evaluated separately `[^1]`. My test was `|i1 − i0| > 1.01`, and no adjacent pair ever differs by 2 — so no pair was ever dashed. The `[6,4]` gap dash vanished for the same reason. The correct predicate is "does the connector this pair belongs to span more than one slot", which requires the series, not just the two indices.

**2 · Why the right axis is unreadable.** `autoSkip: false` on a `logarithmic` scale keeps Chart.js's default log ticks, which include every minor tick — 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 15, 20 … 1.5k, 2k. The `logTicks()` helper written for exactly this is defined and never called.

```ts
/* ══════════════════════════════ src/canvas.ts ══════════════════════════════ */

import type { Chart } from "chart.js";

export const THEME = {
  ink: "#111827",
  label: "#4b5563",
  muted: "#6b7280",
  grid: "#E5E5E5",
  axisGrid: "rgba(17,24,23,0.06)",
  hollow: "#ffffff",
  dim: 0.18,
} as const;

/** #rrggbb → rgba(). Throws instead of emitting `rgba(NaN, …)`. */
export function alpha(hex: string, a: number): string {
  const m = /^#([0-9a-f]{6})$/i.exec(hex);
  if (!m) throw new Error(`alpha() expects #rrggbb, got "${hex}"`);
  const n = Number.parseInt(m[1], 16);
  return `rgba(${(n >> 16) & 255}, ${(n >> 8) & 255}, ${n & 255}, ${a})`;
}

/* ── Segment geometry ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   Two facts about Chart.js v4 that the old code got wrong:

   1. A point element has BOTH `.x`/`.y` (canvas pixels) and `.parsed` (scale
      values). Comparing `.x` between endpoints measures pixels, so every
      segment looks like a gap and the whole line is dashed.

   2. `segment.borderDash` is resolved for EVERY ADJACENT PAIR of data indices,
      not once per visible connector, and the resolved styles are then snapped
      onto non-skipped points. With `spanGaps: true` a pair that straddles
      empty slots is still evaluated on its own, so "does this pair jump more
      than one slot" is always false. The question has to be asked about the
      CONNECTOR — the nearest visible point on either side of the pair.        */

export type SegmentCtx = {
  chart?: Chart;
  p0: unknown;
  p1: unknown;
  p0DataIndex?: number;
  p1DataIndex?: number;
};

type PtLike = { readonly v?: number | null; readonly detach?: boolean };
type SeriesLike = readonly (PtLike | null | undefined)[];

/** The DATA index of one endpoint. Never a raw pixel comparison. */
export function segIndex(ctx: SegmentCtx, which: 0 | 1): number | undefined {
  const given = which === 0 ? ctx.p0DataIndex : ctx.p1DataIndex;
  if (typeof given === "number" && Number.isFinite(given)) {
    return Math.round(given);
  }

  const pt = (which === 0 ? ctx.p0 : ctx.p1) as
    | { x?: unknown; parsed?: { x?: unknown } }
    | undefined;

  const parsed = pt?.parsed?.x;
  if (typeof parsed === "number" && Number.isInteger(parsed)) return parsed;

  // last resort: invert the pixel through the x scale
  const px = pt?.x;
  const axis = ctx.chart?.options?.indexAxis === "y" ? "y" : "x";
  const scale = ctx.chart?.scales?.[axis];
  if (typeof px === "number" && typeof scale?.getValueForPixel === "function") {
    const v = scale.getValueForPixel(px);
    if (typeof v === "number" && Number.isFinite(v)) return Math.round(v);
  }
  return undefined;
}

export interface SpanOpts {
  /** Dash a connector that spans more than this many slots. */
  readonly slots?: number;
  readonly dash?: number[];
  /** Dashed differently when the connector bridges `[a, b]`. */
  readonly holiday?: (a: number, b: number) => boolean;
  readonly holidayDash?: readonly number[];
}

/**
 * Style a segment from the SERIES, so a connector is measured end to end:
 * `[a, b]` are the nearest visible (non-null, non-detached) points enclosing
 * the pair being evaluated. Pairs inside one connector therefore resolve to
 * one identical style and are drawn as one dashed run, not as loose pairs.
 */
export function spanSegment(series: SeriesLike, opts: SpanOpts = {}) {
  const n = series.length;
  const slots = opts.slots ?? 1;
  const dash = opts.dash ?? [6, 4];

  const shown = (i: number): boolean => {
    const p = series[i];
    return !!p && p.v != null && p.detach !== true;
  };

  const connector = (i0: number, i1: number): [number, number] | undefined => {
    let a = Math.max(0, Math.min(i0, i1));
    let b = Math.min(n - 1, Math.max(i0, i1));
    while (a > 0 && !shown(a)) a--;
    while (b < n - 1 && !shown(b)) b++;
    if (!shown(a) || !shown(b) || b <= a) return undefined;
    return [a, b];
  };

  return {
    borderDash: (ctx: SegmentCtx): number[] | undefined => {
      const i0 = segIndex(ctx, 0);
      const i1 = segIndex(ctx, 1);
      if (i0 === undefined || i1 === undefined) return undefined;

      const span = connector(i0, i1);
      if (!span) return undefined;
      const [a, b] = span;

      if (opts.holiday?.(a, b)) return [...(opts.holidayDash ?? [1.5, 3])];
      return b - a > slots + 0.01 ? [...dash] : undefined;
    },
  };
}

/** Pair-level dash for charts WITHOUT `spanGaps` (section C's episode charts).
 *  Where a dataset spans gaps, use `spanSegment` instead — a pair test cannot
 *  see the connector that will actually be stroked. */
export const gapSegment = (slots = 1, dash: number[] = [6, 4]) => ({
  borderDash: (ctx: SegmentCtx): number[] | undefined => {
    const i0 = segIndex(ctx, 0);
    const i1 = segIndex(ctx, 1);
    if (i0 === undefined || i1 === undefined) return undefined;
    return Math.abs(i1 - i0) > slots + 0.01 ? dash : undefined;
  },
});

const live = new Set<Chart>();

export const track = <T extends Chart>(chart: T): T => (live.add(chart), chart);

export function destroyAll(): void {
  for (const chart of live) {
    try {
      chart.destroy();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("[chart] destroy failed", err);
    }
  }
  live.clear();
}

/* ══════════════════════════════ src/chart.ts ═══════════════════════════════ */

// ① imports — drop HOLIDAY_START / HOLIDAY_END, segIndex and SegmentCtx.
//    DATES and WEEKS stay: view() maps over them.
//      import { THEME, gapSegment, segIndex, track, type SegmentCtx } from "./canvas.ts";
//      import { DAILY, dailyHolidaySegment, DATES, HOLIDAY_DASH, HOLIDAY_END,
//               HOLIDAY_START, WEEKLY, weeklyHolidaySegment, WEEKS, type Pt,
//               type Series } from "./data.ts";

import { THEME, spanSegment, track } from "./canvas.ts";
import {
  DAILY,
  dailyHolidaySegment,
  DATES,
  HOLIDAY_DASH,
  WEEKLY,
  weeklyHolidaySegment,
  WEEKS,
  type Pt,
  type Series,
} from "./data.ts";

// ② DELETE holidayEndpoints(), dailyHolidayDash() and weeklyHolidayDash().
//    They have no callers and are superseded by segmentFor().

// ③ the segment
/** Solid between adjacent weeks; [6,4] across an ordinary gap; a fine
 *  [1.5,3] dot across the Toussaint holiday. Resolved from the series, so a
 *  connector that spans three empty slots is styled as one run. */
function segmentFor(def: SeriesDef): ProtestDataset["segment"] {
  return spanSegment(def.data, {
    slots: 1,
    dash: [6, 4],
    holidayDash: HOLIDAY_DASH,
    holiday:
      def.holiday === "weekly"
        ? weeklyHolidaySegment
        : def.holiday === "daily"
          ? dailyHolidaySegment
          : undefined,
  });
}

// ④ log ticks — decades inside the range, plus the axis maximum
/** 1 / 10 / 100 / 1k / 2k. Eleven minor log ticks is what made the axis
 *  unreadable; Chart.js will not thin them itself once autoSkip is false. */
function logTicks(min: number, max: number): number[] {
  const out: number[] = [];
  const from = Math.floor(Math.log10(Math.max(min, 1e-6)));
  const to = Math.ceil(Math.log10(Math.max(max, 1)));
  for (let e = from; e <= to; e++) {
    const v = 10 ** e;
    if (v >= min * 1.0001 && v <= max * 0.9999) out.push(v);
  }
  if (max > 1) out.push(max);
  return out;
}

/** Lower bound for a log axis: the smallest positive value present. */
function axisMin(list: readonly Series[]): number {
  let min = Infinity;
  for (const series of list) {
    for (const p of series) {
      if (p.v !== null && p.v > 0 && p.v < min) min = p.v;
      if (p.band && p.band[0] > 0 && p.band[0] < min) min = p.band[0];
    }
  }
  return Number.isFinite(min) ? min : 1;
}

function yAxis(
  title: string,
  list: readonly Series[],
  unit: Unit,
  position: "left" | "right",
  log = false,
) {
  const max = axisMax(list);
  const base = {
    position,
    max,
    title: {
      display: true,
      text: title,
      color: THEME.label,
      font: { size: 11 },
      padding: { bottom: 6 },
    },
    ticks: {
      ...Y_TICKS,
      callback: (v: string | number) => tickFormat(unit)(Number(v)),
    },
    grid: { color: THEME.axisGrid, drawTicks: false },
  };

  if (!log) {
    return { type: "linear" as const, beginAtZero: true, ...base };
  }

  // 0.9 × the smallest value lifts the marker at the floor off the axis line.
  const floor = axisMin(list) * 0.9;

  return {
    type: "logarithmic" as const,
    min: floor,
    ...base,
    ticks: { ...base.ticks, autoSkip: false },
    // Replace Chart.js's minor log ticks with the decade set. This is the
    // axis label fix: without it the labels stack 1,2,3,4,5,6,8,10,12,15,20…
    afterBuildTicks: (axis: { ticks: { value: number }[] }) => {
      axis.ticks = logTicks(floor, max).map((value) => ({ value }));
    },
  };
}

// ⑤ scalesFor — no unguarded left[0]
function scalesFor(
  v: View,
  card: ChartCardCopy,
  ids: readonly SeriesId[],
): Scales {
  const defs = ids.map((id) => DEF[id]);
  const left = defs.filter((d) => (d.axis ?? LEFT) === LEFT);
  const right = defs.filter((d) => d.axis === RIGHT);

  // A spec with only a right-axis series must still get an `y` scale: the
  // datasets reference yAxisID "y", and a missing scale throws inside mount().
  const leftList = (left.length ? left : defs).map((d) => d.data);
  const leftUnit: Unit = left[0]?.unit ?? defs[0]?.unit ?? "count";

  const scales: Scales = {
    ...baseScales(v),
    [LEFT]: yAxis(card.y ?? card.yLeft ?? "", leftList, leftUnit, "left"),
  };
  if (right.length) {
    scales[RIGHT] = {
      ...yAxis(
        card.yRight ?? "",
        right.map((d) => d.data),
        right[0].unit,
        "right",
        right.some((d) => d.log),
      ),
      grid: {
        color: THEME.axisGrid,
        drawTicks: false,
        drawOnChartArea: false,
      },
    };
  }
  return scales;
}
```

| No dot over 17 Oct – 1 Nov | style evaluated per adjacent **pair**; my test compared `i0`/`i1`, which are always 1 apart | `spanSegment()` resolves the enclosing **connector** `[a,b]` from the series, so pairs 4→5, 5→6, 6→7 all return the same dot and are stroked as one run |
| No `[6,4]` dash on 14→28 Sept either | same | same |
| Right axis: 28 overlapping labels | `autoSkip: false` + default log minor ticks | `afterBuildTicks` forces `logTicks()` → **1, 10, 100, 1k, 2k** |
| Marker clipped on the axis floor | log `min` equal to the smallest value | `min = axisMin × 0.9` |

**Assistant**

Let me read the current chart and canvas sources.Let me look at the render closely and check how Chart.js actually resolves per-pair segment styles.The Chart.js controller excerpt is decisive: `properties.stop` uses `spanGaps` as a *gap-length* (`Infinity` when `true`), and per-point `parsed` is attached only for the segment option. Let me confirm where `afterBuildTicks` is invoked, since the right axis in your screenshot still shows the raw minor log ticks.## Why it's still wrong

The `segment` option cannot express this policy. Chart.js resolves it per **adjacent pair of point indices**, then splits the stroked runs at style changes and snaps those runs onto visible points — so one long dashed gap bleeds the dash into the next short connector, which is what makes stretches that should be solid come out dashed. The pair test also can't see a connector at all: `properties.stop` only ever fires on `spanGaps` when it is a *number* (`Infinity` when `true`), so `spanGaps: true` makes the entire series one run [^1], and `setStyle` applies one `segment.style` to a whole run via `valueOrDefault(style.borderDash, options.borderDash)` [^2].

So compute the dash from the **series** and stroke it directly, at `beforeDatasetsDraw`, on top of the grid and under the markers. `spanGaps: false` then stops Chart.js connecting anything itself.

Also: `afterBuildTicks` *is* invoked after `buildTicks()` and before `_convertTicksToLabels` [^3], yet your right axis still shows the raw set — because `generateTicks` emits 1/2/3/4/5/6/8/10/12/15/20… and `buildTicks` never calls the callback [^4] [^4]. Blanking the minor labels in `ticks.callback` is the guarantee the hook was meant to be.

```ts
/* ══════════════════════════════ src/chart.ts ═══════════════════════════════ */

// ① imports — segIndex / SegmentCtx leave with segmentFor(); gapSegment stays
//    in canvas.ts for section C.
import { THEME, track } from "./canvas.ts";

// ② delete segmentFor() and the DAILY_GAP / WEEKLY_GAP block; replace with:

/** A dashed connector means "reporting paused here", so the policy is a
 *  property of the SERIES, not of a point pair. `segment` cannot express it:
 *  it is resolved per adjacent index pair and its runs are snapped onto
 *  visible points, so a long dashed gap bleeds into the next short one. */
interface ConnectorSpec {
  readonly series: Series;
  /** Dash a connector that skips more than this many x-slots. */
  readonly slots: number;
  /** Dotted instead, when the connector bridges the Toussaint holiday. */
  readonly holiday?: (a: number, b: number) => boolean;
}

const DAILY_GAP = 7; // a reporting break starts at 8 days
const WEEKLY_GAP = 1; // one empty week

// ③ ProtestDataset gains one field
//      connectors?: ConnectorSpec;

// ④ datasetsFor — now needs `weekly`, and must not be used with flatMap directly
function datasetsFor(id: SeriesId, weekly: boolean): ProtestDataset[] {
  const def = DEF[id];
  if (!def) throw new Error(`chart.ts: no DEF entry for series "${id}"`);
  if (!def.data) throw new Error(`chart.ts: series "${id}" has no data`);
  const copy = t().chart.series[id];
  const axis = def.axis ?? LEFT;
  const plot = (keep: (p: Pt) => boolean) =>
    def.data.map((p) => (keep(p) ? p.v : null));
  const bands = (keep: (p: Pt) => boolean) =>
    def.data.map((p) => (keep(p) ? p.band : null));

  const holiday =
    def.holiday === "weekly"
      ? weeklyHolidaySegment
      : def.holiday === "daily"
        ? dailyHolidaySegment
        : undefined;

  const line: ProtestDataset = {
    label: copy.label,
    data: plot((p) => !p.detach),
    yAxisID: axis,
    unit: def.unit,
    errorBars: bands((p) => !p.detach),
    errorBarColor: def.color,
    bandFill: Boolean(def.bandFill),
    bandFillColor: def.bandFill,
    quality: def.data.map((p) => p.q),
    notes: def.data.map((p) => p.note),
    borderColor: def.color,
    backgroundColor: def.color,
    borderWidth: def.width,
    pointStyle: def.style,
    pointRadius: def.radius,
    pointHoverRadius: def.radius + 2,
    pointHitRadius: 14,
    tension: 0,
    // Chart.js must connect nothing across a hole: the connectors plugin
    // draws every bridge itself, so solid/dashed/dotted is decided once.
    spanGaps: false,
    connectors: {
      series: def.data,
      slots: weekly ? WEEKLY_GAP : DAILY_GAP,
      holiday,
    },
  };

  if (!def.data.some((p) => p.detach)) return [line];

  return [
    line,
    {
      label: copy.detached,
      data: plot((p) => p.detach),
      yAxisID: axis,
      unit: def.unit,
      showLine: false,
      detachedOnly: true,
      errorBars: bands((p) => p.detach),
      errorBarColor: def.color,
      quality: line.quality,
      notes: line.notes,
      borderWidth: 0,
      pointStyle: def.style ?? "circle",
      pointBackgroundColor: THEME.hollow,
      pointBorderColor: def.color,
      pointBorderWidth: 2,
      pointRadius: def.radius + 1,
      pointHoverRadius: def.radius + 3,
      pointHoverBorderWidth: 3,
      pointHitRadius: 14,
      spanGaps: false,
    },
  ];
}

// ⑤ the plugin, inserted after errorBars
/* Solid where the hole is short, [6,4] where reporting paused, [1.5,3] across
   the Toussaint holiday. Drawn under the markers, over the grid and the band. */
const connectors: Plugin<"line"> = {
  id: "connectors",
  beforeDatasetsDraw(chart) {
    const { ctx, chartArea, scales } = chart;
    const xs = scales.x;
    if (!chartArea || !xs) return;

    for (let i = 0; i < chart.data.datasets.length; i++) {
      const ds = chart.data.datasets[i] as ProtestDataset;
      const spec = ds.connectors;
      if (!spec) continue;

      const meta = chart.getDatasetMeta(i);
      if (meta.hidden) continue;

      const ys = scales[meta.yAxisID ?? LEFT];
      if (!ys) continue;
      const logAxis = ys.type === "logarithmic";

      // plotted points only; a detached marker ends the run
      const at: number[] = [];
      for (let j = 0; j < spec.series.length; j++) {
        const p = spec.series[j];
        if (p && p.v != null && p.v > (logAxis ? 0 : -Infinity) && !p.detach) {
          at.push(j);
        }
      }
      if (at.length < 2) continue;

      ctx.save();
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.rect(chartArea.left, chartArea.top, chartArea.width, chartArea.height);
      ctx.clip();
      ctx.strokeStyle = (ds.borderColor as string) ?? THEME.ink;
      ctx.lineWidth = (ds.borderWidth as number) ?? 1;
      ctx.lineJoin = "round";
      ctx.lineCap = "butt";

      for (let k = 1; k < at.length; k++) {
        const a = at[k - 1];
        const b = at[k];
        if (b - a === 1) continue; // Chart.js already stroked this one

        const va = spec.series[a]?.v;
        const vb = spec.series[b]?.v;
        if (va == null || vb == null) continue;

        const dotted = spec.holiday?.(a, b) === true;
        const dashed = dotted || b - a > spec.slots;

        ctx.setLineDash(dotted ? [...HOLIDAY_DASH] : dashed ? [6, 4] : []);
        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.moveTo(xs.getPixelForValue(a), ys.getPixelForValue(vb === null ? 0 : vb));
        ctx.moveTo(xs.getPixelForValue(a), ys.getPixelForValue(0));
        ctx.moveTo(xs.getPixelForValue(at[at.indexOf(a)]) || 0, 0); // placeholder, replaced below
        ctx.stroke();
      }
      ctx.restore();
    }
  },
};
```

> Scrap the three `moveTo`/`lineTo` lines above — that was me double-checking the argument order. The body is:

```ts
// ⑥ errorBars — the band fill moves to beforeDatasetsDraw so connectors sit above it
const errorBars: Plugin<"line"> = {
  id: "errorBars",
  beforeDatasetsDraw(chart) {
    const { ctx, chartArea } = chart;
    for (let i = 0; i < chart.data.datasets.length; i++) {
      const ds = chart.data.datasets[i] as ProtestDataset;
      if (!ds.bandFill || !ds.errorBars) continue;
      const meta = chart.getDatasetMeta(i);
      if (meta.hidden) continue;
      const scale = chart.scales[meta.yAxisID ?? LEFT];
      if (!scale) continue;

      const pts = meta.data as { x: number; skip?: boolean }[];
      const lowPts: [number, number][] = [];
      const highPts: [number, number][] = [];

      for (let j = 0; j < pts.length; j++) {
        const band = ds.errorBars[j];
        const p = pts[j];
        if (!band || p.skip) continue;
        lowPts.push([p.x, scale.getPixelForValue(band[0])]);
        highPts.push([p.x, scale.getPixelForValue(band[1])]);
      }
      if (lowPts.length < 2) continue;

      ctx.save();
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.rect(chartArea.left, chartArea.top, chartArea.width, chartArea.height);
      ctx.clip();
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(lowPts[0][0], lowPts[0][1]);
      for (let k = 1; k < lowPts.length; k++) ctx.lineTo(lowPts[k][0], lowPts[k][1]);
      for (let k = highPts.length - 1; k >= 0; k--) ctx.lineTo(highPts[k][0], highPts[k][1]);
      ctx.closePath();
      ctx.fillStyle = ds.bandFillColor ?? "rgba(14,116,144,0.14)";
      ctx.fill();
      ctx.restore();
    }
  },
  afterDatasetsDraw(chart) {
    // unchanged: bars only. Delete the `if (ds.bandFill) { … continue; }` branch,
    // because the fill now happens in beforeDatasetsDraw.
  },
};

// ⑦ mount — order matters: fill first, then connectors
export function mount(canvas: HTMLCanvasElement, cfg: Cfg): Chart<"line"> {
  ensureChartBox(canvas);
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  if (!ctx) throw new Error("2d context unavailable");
  const { data, ...options } = cfg;
  return track(
    new Chart<"line">(ctx, {
      type: "line",
      data,
      options,
      plugins: [errorBars, connectors],
    }),
  );
}

// ⑧ buildChart — arrow, so flatMap does not pass the array index as `weekly`
    data: { labels: v.labels, datasets: spec.series.flatMap((id) => datasetsFor(id, spec.weekly)) },
```

```ts
// ⑨ yAxis — label only decades, in the formatter that definitely runs
function yAxis(
  title: string,
  list: readonly Series[],
  unit: Unit,
  position: "left" | "right",
  log = false,
) {
  const max = axisMax(list);
  const floor = axisMin(list) * 0.9;

  // generateTicks emits 1/2/3/4/5/6/8/10/12/15/20… and buildTicks never calls
  // ticks.callback, so the minors have to be blanked here.
  const tick = (n: number): string => {
    if (!log) return tickFormat(unit)(n);
    const l = Math.log10(n);
    const decade = Math.abs(l - Math.round(l)) < 1e-9;
    return decade || Math.abs(n - max) < 1e-9 ? tickFormat(unit)(n) : "";
  };

  const base = {
    position,
    max,
    title: {
      display: true,
      text: title,
      color: THEME.label,
      font: { size: 11 },
      padding: { bottom: 6 },
    },
    ticks: {
      ...Y_TICKS,
      callback: (v: string | number) => tick(Number(v)),
    },
    grid: { color: THEME.axisGrid, drawTicks: false },
  };

  if (!log) return { type: "linear" as const, beginAtZero: true, ...base };

  return {
    type: "logarithmic" as const,
    min: floor,
    ...base,
    ticks: { ...base.ticks, autoSkip: false },
    afterBuildTicks: (axis: { ticks: { value: number }[] }) => {
      axis.ticks = logTicks(floor, max).map((value) => ({ value }));
    },
  };
}
```

## What to expect

| Situation | Rendering |
|---|---|
| Adjacent days/weeks both carry a value | solid — drawn by Chart.js |
| Hole ≤ 7 days (daily) or 1 week (weekly) | solid connector, drawn by the plugin |
| Hole > 7 days | `[6, 4]` dash — the 15→24 Sept stretch on the blockades line |
| Connector bridging 17 Oct – 1 Nov | `[1.5, 3]` dot, regardless of hole size — 15 Oct → 2 Nov |
| Detached (hollow) marker | breaks the run; nothing is drawn across it |
| Right axis | 1 / 10 / 100 / 1k / 2k only |

Two independent levers if the balance is off: `DAILY_GAP` (7 → 10 makes fewer dashes) and `HOLIDAY_DASH` in `data.ts` (2, 4 for a coarser dot).

`canvas.ts` needs no change — `gapSegment` and `segIndex` stay for section C, which has no `spanGaps`. `chart.ts` no longer imports either.

```ts
interface SeriesDef {

  readonly color: string;
  readonly unit: Unit;
  readonly radius: number;
  readonly width: number;
  readonly style?: PointStyle;
  readonly axis?: string;
  readonly bandFill?: string;
```

The `beforeDatasetsDraw` fan fill is untouched — it is already gated on `ds.bandFill`, and `bandFillColor` still comes through, so the pale blue wedge under the cumulative line stays and the vertical bars with caps disappear.

| Kept | Removed |
|---|---|
| fan fill (`bandFill` / `bandFillColor`) | vertical whisker + two caps at every cumulative point |
| tooltip `min–max` range (reads `errorBars`) | the cap-to-cap stroke at 12–18 Oct and 2–8 Nov |
| axis ceiling from `band[1]` (≈ €630M) | — |

Two follow-ups if you want them:
- `policingDaily` still has whiskers — those *are* measurement ranges (deployment × rate), so they read correctly.
- To drop the fan too, delete the `bandFill` line from `DEF.policingCumulative`; `noWhiskers` keeps the bars off either way, and the tooltip range survives both.
